# Review log: Data lifecycle second half: remaining erasure stores, Phase 3 lease revocation, audit-receipt foreign key, and full_revoke

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass after spec round 6). The pass lifted every FACT, DECISION, WATCHOUT, MISTAKE, OPEN, UNVERIFIED, and unclosed DEFERRED from spec rounds 1 to 6 and merged duplicates that several lenses filed. It deleted these superseded entries:
- The round-1 extended §10.2 single-tenant row, its bootstrap-ordering WATCHOUTs, the "Both rejection cases are logged" wording WATCHOUTs, the spec.2.review-citations WATCHOUT on the cell, the spec.2.review-mechanism CORRECTS, and the spec.2.review-reliability DEFERRED on the httpsurface.go single-tenant branch. Round 2 withdrew the row (spec.2.fix-G1.1).
- The Edit 4e and CODE-8 user-sourced deny-list WATCHOUTs, and the deny-list UNVERIFIED entries in spec.1.review-security and spec.1.review-single-source. Round 1 G2 removed deny-listing from full_revoke.
- The §4.2 duplicate-copy WATCHOUTs (closed by Edit 3d), the "all mutable fields nulled" spec WATCHOUTs and the exempt-flush overclaim (closed by G4), the Edit 5a/5b duplicate keep-sentence WATCHOUT (closed: the rule lives only in step 18), the spec.1.review-citations UNVERIFIED on step 18 (closed by G3), the D16 OPEN on naming `Remove` (closed by spec.1.follow-up-fix), and the bootstrap-token UNVERIFIED in spec.4.review-fresh (answered by spec.4.review-mechanism).
- Owner-answer pass (2026-10-22): retired the issued_tokens GC dispute and filing (D25 wires the collector), the billing-stream TTL dispute (Traps records the departure), the bearer-discriminator UNVERIFIED (CODE-6 item 6 discriminates by verifying key), the OPEN entries OD-1, OD-2, and OD-3 settled as D27, D28, and D26, and every Deferred entry the staging now applies. Corrected the bootstrap seed fact.
- Redesign pass (2026-10-22, owner decisions R1 to R3): retired the Settled "Bootstrap seed on a tombstone (D27 bound)" and "T1.18 wiring row" entries, applied the CORRECTS on "Owner decisions (2026-10-22)", "X1 admin header", "FlusherManager", and "Bootstrap admin in multi-tenant (D27)", amended "Exempt flush claim", and rewrote the T-10.2.11 entries as landed context.
- Redesign-3 pass (2026-10-22): rewrote the Settled entries "`ExtractTenant` single-tenant branch", "Tombstone blocks reuse", and "Step 18 keep rule (G3)", the Traps entries on the withdrawn single-tenant row and on the Phase 3 and §10.2 cross-cite, and retired the Open entry "OPEN full_revoke cancel latency", which the decisions phase closed as OD-5 (accept as staged; see non-spec.7 Standing-context FACT and review-log-archive.md).
- Prune pass (2026-10-22, after non-spec round 12): applied the CORRECTS on "Old §10.2 label" (non-spec.10, non-spec.12) and "Tenant liveness" (non-spec.12.review-edit-sites WATCHOUT), closed the D12 residency UNVERIFIED and the escrow artifact-hold UNVERIFIED, and lifted the round-12 OPEN entries into Open.
- Round 12 log: fix-G1 and fix-design-G1 cut the Tenancy mode paragraph's playground-mint detail to a pointer at row 5; fix-G2 and fix-design-G2 deleted the inbound-citer list from the liveness subsection and moved DOCS-1 item 6 to D28; the review lenses filed no behavior finding, and review-single-source asked for the dev-header and playground-mint restatements to be reduced, which this prune applies.
No Retired section exists in this file, so this changelog records the retirements.

### Settled

- **Edit 3d (§4.2 pointer):** the §4.2 `tenant_id` claim extraction body becomes a one-sentence pointer at §10.2 **`tenant_id` extraction**. It stays as a deduplication pointer after the single-tenant row was withdrawn. (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1)
- **Edit 3a (claim per token type):** the configured claim is read from an OIDC ID token and `tenant_id` from a gateway-issued token. The edit does not name the discriminator. (spec.1.fix-G1.1)
- **Bearer path today:** the auth middleware reads the configured `auth.tenantIdClaim` from every bearer, gateway-minted included, and no mint stamps a custom claim, so a non-default claim name 401s gateway tokens. (pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:469-480)
- **Single-tenant wiring (T-10.2.11 landed):** merge 2177ac596 deleted `permissiveRegistry`. `applyTenantAuthOptions` (cmd/lenny-gateway/tenancy.go) sets `MultiTenant` from `--multi-tenant`, wires `bearerTenantRegistry` only in multi-tenant mode, and sets `DevHeaderNamesTenant` only in single-tenant dev mode, and `validateTenancyConfig` (startupgates.go) refuses a `--multi-tenant` that contradicts `--tenancy-mode`. A single-tenant gateway ignores the bearer's claim and serves `default`. (D26, R3)
- **`ExtractTenant` single-tenant branch:** returns `default` without a registry check. Non-`default` tenants can be created and deleted in single-tenant mode (CODE-6 item 5 refuses only `default`), so a deleted non-`default` tenant's bearers stay accepted there and run as `default`. SPEC-2 Edit 2a scopes the Phase 3 refusal to multi-tenant mode, SPEC-5c relies on no §10.2 refusal, and the non-spec **Tenant liveness and the default tenant** paragraph **Tenancy mode** is the one statement of the single-tenant bound. (pkg/auth/auth.go ExtractTenant; redesign3.2026-10-22)
- **Edit 2b (single-tenant `default` refusal):** a single-tenant deployment refuses DELETE and force-delete of `default` with `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`. Its one home is the §12.8 **Tenant deletion lifecycle.** opening paragraph. It is backed by CODE-6 item 5 and T1.18. (spec.2.fix-G1.1)
- **Edit 2b anchor:** `persisted in Postgres and exposed via the admin API.` occurs once, at the end of the opening sentence of the lifecycle paragraph (spec/12:911).
- **§10.2 single-tenant cell:** stays as the tree has it, because `default` is undeletable in single-tenant mode. (spec.3.review-fresh)
- **Deletion entry points:** only `handleDeleteTenant` and `handleForceDeleteTenant` (via `transitionToDisabling`) start deletion, plus the Phase 1 disabler. PUT does not apply `state`, so the Edit 2b guard has no bypass. (tenants.go:1701, forcedelete.go:182)
- **Force-delete guard:** force-delete starts the lifecycle on an active tenant, so the refusal guards both handlers.
- **Admin Router setting (R2):** the Router already carries `tenancyMode` from `admin.Options.TenancyMode`, which `buildAdminRouter` sets from `--tenancy-mode` (adminrouter.go:295, tenants.go:447,482). CODE-6 item 5 keys the `default` refusal on it; any value other than `multi`, an empty one included, refuses. No setter is added.
- **CODE-6 Targets:** no longer name `adminrouter.go`, because R2 removed the setter wiring; the summary index CODE-6 line matches. (redesign.2026-10-22)
- **`INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`:** catalogued PERMANENT 409. The §15.1 tenant DELETE and force-delete rows, §24 rows, and §25 tool rows list no error set, so Edit 2b falsifies none. (spec/15:847,897,1011)
- **`default` row:** with Postgres, migrations 0053 and 0054 insert the `default` tenants row before the `lenny-bootstrap` Job runs, and the Job's upsert finds it. (migrations/0053_connectors_tenant_scoped.up.sql:34-35, 0054:27-28)
- **Bootstrap admin token tenant:** the `lenny-admin` token is minted under `--admin-token-tenant`, default `default`, in every mode. (flags.go:1295)
- **Owner decisions (2026-10-22):** D26 (T-10.2.11 direct fix first, now landed in merge 2177ac596; X1 runs `--tenancy-mode=multi --multi-tenant=true`, because `--multi-tenant=true` alone exits at startup with `LENNY_CONFIG_INVALID`), D27 (multi-tenant `default` deletable; Edit 2b and DOCS-1 item 6 record the consequences), D28 (the `default` refusal reads `TenancyMode`, and an unset value is single-tenant).
- **Redesign decisions (2026-10-22):** R1, the bootstrap seed refuses a tenant in `deleting` or `deleted` as an `error` with and without `--force-update` (D29, CODE-6 item 8, T1.24, S11), which removes the only path that clears `DeletedAt` and retires the revival bound. R2, the `default` refusal reads `Options.TenancyMode` and no `WithMultiTenant` setter exists (D28, T1.18). R3, T-10.2.11 is landed context (D26, checklist precondition satisfied).
- **Tenant liveness:** the non-spec subsection **Tenant liveness and the default tenant** is the one home for the liveness predicate (listed, registered, accepts new work, and the missing-`default` fallback), the per-site table, and the `tenantsLister` caller table. Its table rows are the only place a site's behavior is written, and its paragraph **Tenancy mode** is the only place for the single-tenant bound and the not-staged reasons; summary D11 and the summary **New** entries describe a defect and point. `grep -n "Tenant liveness"` across the proposal lists the citers; do not keep an inbound-citer list here or in the subsection (non-spec.12.fix-G2). Check a liveness finding against its rows before filing. (redesign.2026-10-22, prune 2026-10-22)
- **X1 admin header:** with `--tenancy-mode=multi --multi-tenant=true`, `applyTenantAuthOptions` wires the registry and the dev-header path runs `ExtractTenant` with it, so `adminDo` must send `default` rather than `acme`. (tenant_deletion_phase4_test.go:390, cmd/lenny-gateway/tenancy.go, pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go `devHeaderTenant`)
- **`platform` reservation:** SPEC-3 Edit 3e and CODE-6 item 7 refuse the ID at both create paths (`handleCreateTenant`, `upsertTenants`). The Store table `audit` guard stays, because Part A does not wait for CODE-6.
- **Bearer registry read:** `bearerTenantRegistry.IsRegistered` is an uncached per-request Postgres primary read. A lookup error maps to refusal. SPEC-3 adds no new hot-path load. (auth_seams.go:97-110)
- **Shipped `IsActive`:** only `DeletedAt.IsZero()`, so today a `deleting` tenant's bearers pass until soft delete. SPEC-2 and SPEC-3 refuse them from Phase 3. (tenantstore.go:237)
- **`TENANT_NOT_ACTIVE`:** stays reachable through `disabling`, so its catalog row stays true. (spec/15:1130)
- **`TENANT_NOT_FOUND` sites:** §10.2 (238, 251, 271), §4.2 (reduced by Edit 3d), §27 (65, 107, 134). Edit 3c covers 107. Docs: client-guide/authentication.md:81, operator-guide/security.md:92.
- **No Tenant CRD:** `TenantState` is a Postgres column. The deletion controller is a leader-gated Runnable in the gateway, polling every 30s. Edit 3c removes the only "provisioned `Tenant` CR" phrase.
- **Kubernetes lens:** no findings in rounds 1, 4, and 6. No staged edit touches a CRD, status subresource, finalizer, or webhook.
- **full_revoke lease step (G2):** writes no credential deny-list entry for any source. Edit 4e is deleted and SPEC-4 edits only §11.4. Step 1 says the return "revokes the lease ([Section 4.9])" and SPEC-2 drops its trailing clause.
- **Deny-list lifetime:** an entry lives only while an active lease exists on its credential. The sweep removes it otherwise, every `--credential-lease-gc-interval-seconds` (default 3600). (credential_lease_sweep.go)
- **Deny-list rebuild:** the §4.9 startup rebuild seeds user entries only from TokenStore `status='revoked'` rows with an active lease. full_revoke never sets that status.
- **Deny-list keys:** per credential (`{source, poolId, credentialId}` / `{source, tenantId, credentialRef}`), never per lease.
- **§4.9 populators:** only the pool revoke endpoint and `POST /v1/credentials/{credential_ref}/revoke`. No spec site names full_revoke, so dropping deny-listing needs no §4.9 edit. (spec/04:1794-1795)
- **Lease revocation basis:** the §4.9 "Leases are revocable" bullet and the LLM Proxy paragraph state a revoked lease is rejected. Spec vocabulary treats release as revocation (spec/06:26, spec/07:447).
- **Proxy lease check:** `pgstore.GetByToken` is a direct per-request Postgres read with no replica cache. A query error answers LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID. Release rejects the token fleet-wide. (pgstore.go:176-181, handler.go:322-328)
- **Silent store errors:** `pgstore.Remove` discards its Exec error and `LeasesBySession` swallows query errors. `credassign.Client.Release` removes the record even when the RevokeCredentials RPC fails.
- **D16 and Remove:** the summary (D16, watch-out bullet, non-goal, **New** defect entry) names `LeasesBySession` and `Remove`. (spec.1.follow-up-fix)
- **0093 impact row:** reads "SPEC-4 and CODE-7 remove the lease step's deny-list write".
- **`leaserevoke.New` callers:** adminrouter.go buildAdminRouter, user_revocation_rotation_test.go, release_wiring_test.go (string row), leaserevoke_test.go, tier4 full_revoke_two_replica, tier8 full_revoke_denylist_drop, tier9 full_revoke_lease_return.
- **full_revoke response:** an untyped map; OpenAPI declares 200 with no schema, and no docs page documents the body. The lease-count change has no client schema to update. (users.go:702-712)
- **full_revoke docs:** docs/api/admin.md:845 is one sentence. The §11.4 Note is the only home for full_revoke outcomes.
- **Repeat after crash:** the coordination Sweeper's `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases bindings of sessions the first call cancelled. (coordination.go:533)
- **Issued-token retention rule (G3, rehomed in redesign3):** an `issued_tokens` row is kept, revoked or not, until its `exp` precedes the §13.3 trailing hour. Its one home is SPEC-5 Edit 5d in §13.3 **Authoritative durability for revocation**, which covers user erasure, tenant Phase 4, and the expired-row GC. Edits 5a, 5b (step 18), and 5c, spec Edge case 2, the spec Design bullet, D9, D25, CODE-4 items 3 and 4, the CODE-4 index line, and the Store table cite it.
- **Why rows are kept:** auth checks only the in-memory cache, `Revoke` returns ErrNotFound on no row, and `RevokeBySubject` finds nothing, so a deleted row makes later revocation a no-op. (auth.go:464, issuedtokenstore.go:348-363,441)
- **§13.3 rehydration window:** `revoked_at IS NOT NULL AND exp > now() - 1h`; cache miss reads `issued_tokens.revoked_at` by jti. (spec/13:674-676)
- **User eraser today:** "tokens" wires `issuedtokenstore.DeleteByUser`, which deletes every row by sub. (adminrouter.go:566)
- **Tombstone keep (G4):** Edit 5c states a predicate (keep every mutable field a resumed or later phase, or a tombstone-snapshot reader, still reads) with a "such as" list of `billingErasurePolicy`, `workspaceTier`, `dataResidencyRegion`. CODE-3 item 6 is the exhaustive keep list (T1.7).
- **Tombstone readers:** Phase 4a reads `workspaceTier` after Phase 4; §11.7 item 3 reads `dataResidencyRegion` from the snapshot. (spec/12:920, spec/11:447)
- **Tombstone blocks reuse:** the retained `tenants` row prevents tenant ID reuse, so no new tenant inherits a deleted tenant's ID, its bearers, or the issued-token rows Phase 4 keeps under the §13.3 retention rule. In multi-tenant mode the deleted tenant stays unregistered, so its bearers stay refused; in single-tenant mode they run as `default` (**Tenancy mode**).
- **Exempt billing (G4):** the Edit 5c stream step is "skipped for a tenant whose `billingErasurePolicy` is `exempt`". Spec Edge case 3 was deleted and items 4 and 5 renumbered to 3 and 4.
- **FlusherManager:** lists via `tenantsLister`. Today `tenantsLister` prepends `default` unconditionally, so a deleted `default` kept its flusher (non-spec.7.fix-design-G1.1). After CODE-3 item 7 it adds `default` only when no row exists, and after CODE-6 item 8 no path clears `DeletedAt`, so every tombstoned stream, `default`'s included, is never flushed again. (worker_seams.go:68-83, billing_security_workers.go:68)
- **Billing stream TTL in spec:** §11.2.1 states `billingStreamTTLSeconds`; §12.4 gives the key 3600s. Code sets none (Traps, **Billing stream TTL departure**).
- **Edge case numbering:** every "Edge case N" reference names the non-spec list. Nothing cites spec-changes Edge cases by number.
- **Edit 6a (G1, round 4):** find-string starts at "as an authorized export and the post-Phase-4 deletion ..." and the replacement drops the `redacted_gdpr` clause, ending "recognizes the escrow record as an authorized export. Phase 4 deletes the original rows ...". Occurs once (spec/12:930).
- **`redacted_gdpr`:** §11.7 defines it only for the DeleteByUser step-14 in-place redaction with a signed RedactionReceipt. The §12.8 `gdpr.*` exemption has the verifier skip `deleting`/`deleted` tenants. (spec/11:384, spec/12:883)
- **Escrow marker sites:** `legal_hold_escrow: true` and "skip logic" appear only at spec/12:919, 930, 931, 933, covered by 5c and 6a-6d.
- **Escrow release after Phase 4:** release reads the durable ledger (ActiveForSession/ActiveForArtifact), so deleting originals does not strand escrow objects. For an artifact hold, `setArtifactLegalHold` answers a catalog miss on hold:false by calling `ReleaseForArtifact` with the request's tenant and artifact ID, and no path deletes `legal_hold_escrow_records`, so a clear on a tombstoned tenant releases the escrow object; T1.20 pins it. (legalhold.go setArtifactLegalHold, release.go:70-93; closes the spec.6.review-mechanism UNVERIFIED)
- **D12 residency:** the CMP-058 residency gate maps an unknown target tenant to the global platform pool, and a tombstoned tenant routes by its kept `dataResidencyRegion`, so CODE-6 item 4's platform-chain TENANT_NOT_FOUND row never fails closed on residency. (non-spec.7.review-citations FACT, non-spec.5.review-security FACT; closes the spec.1.review-edit-sites UNVERIFIED)
- **Erasure salt:** envelope-encrypted in the tenant row with no KMS key of its own. D19's removal of ScheduleKeyDeletion is consistent; §18 stays true. (spec/12:891)
- **Receipt index:** `audit_redaction_receipts` carries UNIQUE (tenant_id, sequence_number), so dropping the FK (SPEC-1) keeps the lookup indexed. Edit 1a matches §16.1. (migration 0160)
- **`billing.retentionDays`:** defined in §11.2.1 (spec/11:164).
- **Access-token cache:** `accessCache` has no production writer (`_ = w.accessCache`), so spec Edge case 3 holds. (lenny-token-service/server.go:94-101)
- **Deletion job store:** in-memory. `ensureJob` rebuilds from `TenantState`, and a `deleting` row always resumes at Phase 3, so a failover mid-Phase-4 reruns Phases 3, 3.5, and 4. (tenantdeletion.go:286-335)
- **Escrow Migrate:** `Migrate` reads every held original before writing escrow and consults no ledger record, although spec/12:918 requires the ledger skip. (migrator.go:203-240)
- **Phase 4 order:** staged SPEC-5c matches the non-spec Store table ranks (tokens 180, issued_tokens 185, credential_pool 190; user_roles 210 to tenant_config 240; audit 130, billing 150, eval_results 155). Check this first when either side changes.
- **Creation fence:** any `TenantState` other than `active` keeps the fence closed.
- **Build order:** tenant deletion is Phase 13 and full_revoke is Phase 7, so no inversion. (spec/18:386,564)
- **Sharding:** tenant-wide listings go through `AllSessionShards()`; its query_type enum has no full_revoke entry, which is pre-existing and inert in v1.
- **Plan conformance:** P5b is the single bullet at gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:2877-2890. Every scope item is staged or declined with a reason (D13, D21, D22, D23, `redis_caches`, `user_roles` exemption). No findings in rounds 1, 4, and 6.
- **Anchor sweeps:** rounds 1, 2, 4, and 6 found every staged find-string and link anchor unique and verbatim, including the widened Edit 6a and the four-space step-14 indent. Re-check only hunks changed since.
- **Edit 5a scope:** "replace the `TokenStore` row" is unambiguous only inside the erasure-scope table; §12.2 has a second `TokenStore` row. (spec/12:13 vs 823)
- **Old §10.2 label:** "`tenant_id` extraction from OIDC tokens" is cited by the code and test files CODE-6 item 9 lists, and CODE-6 item 9 retargets them; no docs, schema, or anchor-form reference exists. (spec.10 CORRECTS, non-spec.12.review-applicability)
- **Single-source lens:** no findings in rounds 4 and 6. Clause-level overlaps already weighed: escrow-original deletion at 5c/6a/6d, Edge cases 1/3/4 vs D11/D13/Non-goals, Edit 1a's locate sentence, the Phase 3 rerun clause, and 6b/6c ledger mentions.
- **Reliability lens round 6:** no spec finding. Each traced defect is code-only or bounded by an existing reclaimer (lease TTL and sweep, claimOrphanTimeout GC, Phase 4's second TokenStore delete).
- **Docs lens round 6:** no spec finding. Accepted edge cases land in staged text, are REC-1 departures, or were refuted.
- **Spec Edge case 4 (crash mid-full_revoke):** round 4 filed it as resolving to no landing text; Edit 4d's Note covers only a client disconnect.
- **`POST /v1/admin/legal-hold`:** admits `platform-admin`, so spec Edge case 1's remedy is reachable in multi-tenant mode.

### Traps

- **Single-tenant `default` registration check (MISTAKE, withdrawn option):** round 1 extended the §10.2 single-tenant row to refuse requests when `default` is not registered. Every single-tenant request, bootstrap and platform-admin included, runs as `default`, so the check refused the bootstrap seed, both Phase 3.5 remedies, the 410 tombstone GET, and every request after the tombstone. Do not reintroduce any registry or state check on `default` in that cell, and do not wire `bearerTenantRegistry` into the single-tenant branch of httpsurface.go. Also rejected then: a "deleting or deleted" predicate (closes bootstrap only), scoping Edge case 1 to multi-tenant as a substitute for the cell, dropping the cell with no refusal, a new error code, and §15.1 rows as a second home. Since redesign3, spec Edge case 1 and Edit 2a are scoped to multi-tenant mode because the refusal itself is multi-tenant only (D11); that is not the rejected substitute.
- **CODE-6 rule 3:** admits a missing `default` row. With Postgres the row always exists (migrations 0053 and 0054), so the admission covers only the in-memory tenant store and a pre-migration state. It stays; do not move it into spec.
- **Mode switch:** deleting `default` in multi-tenant mode and then switching to single-tenant serves a tombstoned tenant. Mode switching is unspecified pre-deployment; rejected as speculative (spec.3.review-fresh).
- **Single-tenant claim honouring:** before merge 2177ac596 the single-tenant wiring honoured the bearer's claim. T-10.2.11 removed that (D26). Do not stage anything for it, and do not re-file it.
- **Bootstrap admin in multi-tenant (D27):** deleting `default` in multi-tenant mode refuses the `lenny-admin` bearer from Phase 3 onward, permanently. Edit 2b states it and DOCS-1 item 6 holds the operator steps. Do not extend the single-tenant refusal to multi-tenant mode.
- **No deny-list populator in full_revoke or Phase 3:** do not reintroduce one to "fail closed". A post-Release user entry is not seeded by the rebuild and is dropped by the sweep once no active lease remains, so it covered only failure paths until restart. Rejected: keeping the populator with a narrowed §4.9 guarantee; marking the credential revoked in TokenStore; deny-listing before Release (revoke hook rotates live direct leases, the 0093 ordering hazard); a per-user key dimension.
- **`leaserevoke.DenyList` survives:** `poolCredentialRevoker` (credential_revocation.go) and the compile-time assertion in user_revocation.go consume it. CODE-7 moves it beside `poolCredentialRevoker`. Dropping it from Revoker changes `New`'s signature, so CODE-7 must name every caller, including the string-pinned row in release_wiring_test.go.
- **No LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID in spec:** §4.9's LLM Proxy paragraph is the home step 1 cites for rejection. Do not name the code in spec text.
- **Silent `Remove` vs Edit 4d:** a lease-store `Remove` error in full_revoke is silent with no deny-list backstop, so code diverges from the Note's "reports it as partial-failure detail". It is the recorded D16/D23 departure (REC-1). Do not re-file in the spec loop.
- **CODE-7 item 3 scope:** names only a `LeasesBySession` listing error. It is narrower than D16 and Edge case 1 but true; a non-spec round may widen it to `Remove`.
- **release_wiring_test.go shared:** CODE-7 edits the `leaserevoke.New(...)` row in the same file that CODE-3, CODE-4, and CODE-5 add rows to. CODE-6 adds no row since R2.
- **Step 18 keep qualifier (MISTAKE):** the pre-round step 18 kept only revoked rows in the window, deleting live unrevoked tokens' rows and breaking §13.3 rotation and full_revoke step 5 for an erased user. Do not reintroduce "revoked" as the qualifier. Do not write the one-hour window in staged spec text; §13.3 owns it and only CODE-4 item 3's SQL carries it. T2.6's `Revoke` of the kept unrevoked row is the discriminating test. Rejected: revoke-then-keep (turns erasure into §11.4 invalidation), dropping TokenIssuanceStore from the user path.
- **`DeleteByUser` doc comment:** claims "a deleted token can no longer be presented for validation", which is false because validation never reads the row. CODE-4 item 3 replaces it. (issuedtokenstore.go:600-608)
- **Exempt flush claim:** do not restore "exempt staged events flush to EventStore" in spec staging. The flusher stops at the tombstone for every tenant, `default` included, once CODE-3 item 7 and CODE-6 item 8 land; non-spec Edge case 5 is the residue's home. Rejected: a spec residue clause, FlusherManager with IncludeDeleted (goroutine per tombstone, could recreate a destroyed stream), drain-then-destroy for exempt tenants, and an Edge case 5 clause accepting a revived tombstone's stream (R1 removes the revival).
- **Tombstone list:** do not replace the predicate with the reviewer's two-field list (contradicts CODE-3 item 6 on WorkspaceTier and ForceDelete*) or copy the full keep list into spec (second home).
- **`redacted_gdpr` for Phase 4:** never describe Phase 4 deletion as a `redacted_gdpr` discontinuity. A real deletion has no RedactionReceipt, so the verifier would raise `broken` and AuditRedactionReceiptMissing would fire. Anchor Edit 6a at "as an authorized export" to avoid "export . Phase 4".
- **Unconditional escrow-original deletion:** Edits 5c and 6a read as unconditional, but Phase 4 still skips `gdpr.*` rows (spec/12:883). The specific rule governs; not filed.
- **Edit 4d "those sessions" (status unrecorded):** round 1 found that dropping "non-terminal" from the Note made "cancels those sessions" cover terminal sessions, contradicting Edit 4b. No later round re-raised it and no fix entry exists in this window. Confirm before relying on either reading.
- **Platform-chain contention (D12):** CODE-6 item 4 puts every TENANT_NOT_FOUND auth_failure row on the single platform chain, which is serialized by an advisory lock (about 20 writes/s per chain at the 50ms P99). A deleting tenant's clients (about 100 RPS at Tier 3) contend with Phase 3.5 `legal_hold.escrowed` writes. §13.3 and §11.6 sample rejection storms for this reason. §11.7 enumerates platform-tenant events narrowly, and no staged edit states D12's placement.
- **Registry staleness:** the bearer registry has no cache, so SPEC-3's "from then on" holds fleet-wide on commit. Do not file a staleness finding.
- **Phase 3 and §10.2 cross-cite:** SPEC-2 Phase 3 and SPEC-3b each cite the other for "a deleting tenant is not registered"; §10.2 is the home. Keep Phase 3 to a one-clause pointer. SPEC-5c's `TokenIssuanceStore` entry carries no §10.2 reason; it cites the §13.3 retention rule.
- **Billing stream TTL departure:** code sets no expiry on `t:{tenant_id}:billing:stream` (only XADD MAXLEN) while spec states one. Non-spec Edge case 5 is filed as a departure through REC-1 item 6, so it is not a spec gap. (redisstream.go:191-205)
- **Force-delete ordering:** force-delete checks legal holds before any state write, so "before any state write" alone does not order the 409 ahead of TENANT_DELETE_BLOCKED_BY_LEGAL_HOLD. Judged specific-over-general; not filed.
- **Phase 3 rerun clause:** "a job resumed at Phase 3 runs them again" is true of the tree. Do not file it against "resumes from the last incomplete phase"; that departure predates 0095.
- **Terminal-session listing cost:** listing terminal sessions (Edit 4a, SPEC-2) is O(lifetime sessions), matching the existing Phase 4 and step-16 pattern. Do not file without a spec-stated budget it breaks.
- **Pre-existing, do not file:** TENANT_NOT_FOUND has no §15.1 catalog row; the §10.2 playground table lists it as 401 against 403; the chart uses `tenancy.mode` against §10.2's `auth.multiTenant`; §12.2's `TokenStore` row omits registered credentials; docs multi-tenancy.md:345 says the tombstone is kept "after Phase 6" against spec Phase 4.
- **Receipt purge:** no spec text says who purges `audit_redaction_receipts` after `audit.gdprRetentionDays`. Edit 1a's "never delete receipts" is consistent; do not call it false without a spec site where the sweep deletes receipts.
- **Tenant receipt counts:** the Phase 6 receipt records timestamps and errors without per-store counts, so non-spec Edge case 4 contradicts no spec text.
- **Plan-scope boundary:** dossier SG-9 and 0094's hand-offs (`session_tree_archive.root_session_id` FK, user registry row) are outside plan-conformance scope.
- **Edit 4e history:** older refutations cite Edit 4e and user-credential deny-listing. Neither exists now; read them as history.
- **Checklist drift:** stale S-step wording is reconciled between loops (see Deferred), not a spec-loop finding.
- **Docs sites belong to DOCS-1:** multi-tenancy.md:337 (`RevokeByTenant`), :345, security.md, and client-guide rows are non-spec-loop work; do not file them in the spec loop.

### Open

- **UNVERIFIED Phase 3 lease query size:** `forEachTenantSession` passes every session ID to one `session_id = ANY($1)` query; batching is a code-loop question. (spec.1.review-performance.1)
- **UNVERIFIED renewal race:** a renewal racing step 1 can mint a lease after the listing that lives until the step-2 cancel releases it. (spec.2.review-security.1)
- **UNVERIFIED CODE-9 item 5 status:** a listing failure answers 500 while the Note names no status; docs loop checks docs/api/admin.md. (spec.6.review-client-surface.1)
- **OPEN single-tenant playground revocation marker:** a playground bearer minted for a deleted tenant in single-tenant mode has its `t:{tenant}:pg:revoked:{jti}` marker written under that tenant and read under `default`, so revocation may miss it. Decide whether the summary row-5 **New** entry names it. (non-spec.12.fix-G1, non-spec.12.fix-design-G1)
- **OPEN DOCS-1 governing list:** the governing-decisions sentence after DOCS-1 item 8 omits D29, which item 6 cites. (non-spec.12.fix-G2)

### Deferred

None.

## Ledger

Two review runs each wrote a non-spec round 7. The `non-spec.7.review-*` blocks between `[non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1]` and `[non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]` belong to the earlier run, which reviewed T1.20 before the owner decisions; the `non-spec.7.*` blocks after `[non-spec.6.review-test-coverage.1]` belong to the later run's round 7, which reviewed T1.12, T2.8, and X1.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: T1.5 asserts that the stream is purged before the durable rows and that the tenant's buffered events are dropped, and it pins no position for the buffer drop — BECAUSE CODE-2 item 3 (the home) states only that the stream purge comes before the primary delete and that the buffer drop is kept — ALTERNATIVES: "then drops the buffered events" was rejected because it pins a buffer position the home does not state; moving the buffer drop in CODE-2 was rejected because no finding motivates that change.
FACT: today `Pipeline.DeleteByTenant` runs `p.primary.DeleteByTenant` first and drops the Tier 2 buffer only after the primary delete returns without error — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/billing/billingstore/failover/failover.go:402-421
WATCHOUT: do not add a buffer-drop ordering rule to CODE-2 or T1.5. The staged order is stream, then primary, then buffer, and any test that asserts "stream, buffer, rows" fails against the code — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 item 3; failover.go:402-421

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]

DECISION: T2.6's last sentence now asserts a keep pass (both kept rows' `exp` passed by less than an hour, nothing deleted) and a delete pass (past the trailing hour, both deleted), plus a `release_wiring_test.go` row pinning the `sweepIssuedTokens` start — BECAUSE `issuedtokenstore.DeleteExpired` deletes every `exp <= cutoff` row, so a delete-only test passes with a `now()` or `now() + 1h` cutoff that deletes step 18's kept rows fleet-wide, and a direct call cannot catch an unstarted sweep — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "exp inside the trailing hour" wording (a future-`exp` unrevoked row cannot tell `now()` from `now() - 1h`); a new T-number (restates T2.6's setup); rewording CODE-4 item 4 (already the home of the cutoff and start site).
WATCHOUT: the keep case discriminates only if both kept rows' `exp` sits in (now-1h, now] at the pass; the sweep computes its cutoff in Go from `clockinject.Now()` (as `sweepIdempotencyKeys` does), so `clockinject.SetOffsetForTest` advances it against real Postgres. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:311; pkg/clockinject/clockinject.go:81
WATCHOUT: `sweepIdempotencyKeys` is started inline (an initial pass plus a ticker goroutine inside `if w.pgPool != nil`), so the wiring row's `want` must be a string unique to the new sweep's call, such as its ticker-loop call on `w.watchdogCtx`. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:237-256
USEFUL [non-spec.1 review-log FACT at :799]: `issuedtokenstore.DeleteExpired` has no production caller (billing_security_workers.go:313 is the idempotency store); re-verified this round.

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]

FACT: `treebudget.PurgeRoot` is a full SCAN (COUNT 256) of the node that owns the root's slot, issued once per call; it is not slot-local, because Redis SCAN cannot be restricted to one slot. Cost per call is (keys on that node)/256 round trips, whether or not the root has keys. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:523, pkg/redisconn/scan.go:17,97-124
FACT: the delegation budget key set of a tree is deterministic: five tree-wide keys plus `parallel_children:{p}` and `children_total:{p}` per distinct ParentSessionID in the tree. `RearmTree` already derives it with no SCAN, and `distinctParents` derives the parent list. Keys carry an 8-day GC TTL. EVIDENCE: treebudget.go:39-50,226-255,480-500; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/budget_rearm.go:30-48
FACT: `sessionstore` `List` has no LIMIT, and Phase 2's `DrainSessions` already materializes every tenant session twice, so listing every session is a pre-existing cost. Calling PurgeRoot for each listed root is new in CODE-3 item 4. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:660-661; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:83-85,121-122
FACT: the tenant-deletion runner reconciles every job serially inside one tick, and a tick runs one phase to completion with no deadline. A long Phase 4 on one tenant therefore stalls every other tenant's deletion. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:236-285; pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:275-290
CORRECTS [Standing context Open, "UNVERIFIED issued_tokens GC wiring"]: billing_security_workers.go:313 is `sweepIdempotencyKeys` calling `idempgstore.DeleteExpired`. `issuedtokenstore.DeleteExpired` (issuedtokenstore.go:582) has no production caller, so CODE-4 item 4 is the first wiring of it. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:305-316
FACT: `bearerTenantRegistry` and `playgroundTenantRegistry` read through `tenantstore.Get`, which unwraps `erasure_salt` through KMS whenever a salt is present. The salt is transient: the erasure job sets it and nulls it. CODE-6 item 3 therefore adds no steady KMS load. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:267-281,98-114; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/billing.go:178,200
WATCHOUT: the user path (`buildSessionScopedErasers`, `delegation_budget`) already calls PurgeRoot for every session of the user, non-roots included, which is one full SCAN each. It is pre-existing and "Unchanged" in the Store table. A fix to the tenant entry should not claim to change it. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:1056-1068

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]

No findings this round. The only change since the snapshot is T1.20, and it matches the tree: legalhold.go:269-293 releases on catalog-not-found, answers 404 on zero releases, and answers 500 on a release error with no audit event.

FACT: every lease-step store call takes no context. `RevokeUserLeases` has no ctx parameter, and pgstore `LeasesBySession` and `Remove` run on `context.Background()` with no timeout. No session-store or tenant-store call sets a per-call deadline, and the repo sets no pool-wide `statement_timeout`; the audit advisory lock is the only exception. So CODE-9 item 1's "Each store call keeps its own bound" is true only in the sense of "whatever bound it has", which is none beyond kernel TCP timeouts. tenant_suspend.go:112 has the same posture. I judged this not material and did not file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/leaserevoke/leaserevoke.go:83, pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:283,308
FACT: tenantstore pgstore `Get` decrypts `erasure_salt` through KMS `UnwrapDEK` on every call (no DEK cache), and both registry adapters call `Get` on every bearer. The salt exists only during a user-erasure billing step or after `RotateErasureSalt`, so the per-request KMS cost is rare. It predates this proposal for non-default tenants, and CODE-6 item 3 extends it to `default`. Not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:98-114,267-281; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/billing.go:176-205
FACT: a Phase 3.5 escrow error other than region-unresolvable routes to `r.fail` (terminal). A failed in-memory job is rebuilt only on leader handoff, because ensureJob returns early when a job exists. CODE-5's new fail-closed listing and lookup errors therefore wedge until a handoff. The summary's **New** entry "Phases other than 2, 3, and 4 still fail terminally" covers this, and it replaces a fail-open drop, so it is not a regression. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:319-328,609-621; cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:291-305
FACT: `credential_leases.session_id` is indexed (migration 0129:95), so the Phase 3 `ANY($1)` lease listing is an index probe. The existing Phase 3.5 hold enumerator already lists every tenant session and makes one catalog call per session, so CODE-8 and CODE-3 add no new listing pattern. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:156-176
WATCHOUT: a paused Phase 4 (Edge case 4) reruns the session-keyed entries every interval. That includes one node SCAN per root session in `delegation_budget`. Treat this as a close variant of the refuted delegation_budget SCAN finding and do not re-file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:518-530
USEFUL [Settled: Bearer registry read]: confirmed that pgstore `Get` reads `s.pool`, the primary, so the Phase 3 refusal takes effect fleet-wide on commit.

WATCHOUT: a tenant named `platform` keeps its own audit rows after deletion (receipt shows `audit: 0`); this is the bounded consequence recorded in the summary's New entry, not a defect to re-file. Do not add a "skipped" sentinel to the count map for it.

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]

FACT: `issuedtokenstore.DeleteExpired` has no production caller at the tree; billing_security_workers.go:313 is `idempgstore` (the idempotency GC). This closes the Open UNVERIFIED on issued_tokens GC wiring in favour of round 1. CODE-4 item 4 is the first caller. EVIDENCE: grep of DeleteExpired over cmd/ and pkg/; cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:238-245,305-316
FACT: `sweepIdempotencyKeys`, which CODE-4 item 4 copies, runs on every replica with no leader gate. It makes a synchronous startup pass on context.Background() and then ticks, and it issues one unbatched `DELETE ... WHERE tenant_id=$1 AND exp<=$2` per listed tenant. `tenantsLister` always prepends "default", so a deployment with a `default` row sweeps it twice per pass, which is harmless. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:236-256; cmd/lenny-gateway/worker_seams.go:72-83; pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go:582-598
FACT: the deletion runner ticks on the process context and checks `IsLeader` only at tick start. A replica that loses leadership mid-tick finishes its pass while the new leader rebuilds the job at Phase 3, so Phase 3 and 4 can run on two replicas at once. The staged design is safe under this: CODE-8 item 1 step 1 skips a row that is already `deleting` or `deleted`, the CODE-5 item 6 ledger skip covers a Phase 3.5 re-entry racing a Phase 4 prefix delete, `tenantstore` pgstore `Get` returns soft-deleted rows, and the tombstone keep list preserves `BillingErasurePolicy` and the override fields. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:236-283; pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:267-281
FACT: Phase 3.5 errors other than a legal-hold block and an unresolvable escrow region still go to `r.fail`, which is terminal until the leader restarts or `Retry` runs. That behavior is pre-existing (`ActiveTenantHolds` and escrow write errors already took this path). The new CODE-5 item 5 and item 6 error returns add Postgres-dependent triggers to the same path, and CODE-8 item 4 pauses only Phase 3 and Phase 4. I did not file it because the shipped tree already fails Phase 3.5 terminally on a Postgres error. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:307-328,609-621
WATCHOUT: today the bearer and playground registries short-circuit `default` with no read. CODE-6 item 3 makes every `default`-tenant bearer request in multi-tenant mode do an uncached `tenantstore.Get` on the primary, which also runs a KMS `UnwrapDEK` when the row holds a rotated erasure salt. Non-default tenants already pay this cost. I did not file it: the cost equals the shipped non-default posture, and §13.3 already fails closed when Postgres is unreachable. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/auth_seams.go:97-110; cmd/lenny-gateway/playground_seams.go:25-37; pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:98-114
USEFUL [Traps: Terminal-session listing cost]: it saved re-filing the O(lifetime sessions) listings in Phase 3, Phase 4 forEachTenantSession, and full_revoke. The Phase 3.5 hold enumerator already lists every tenant session (tenantdeletion.go:157), so the pattern predates this proposal.

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]

FACT: `platform` is not a reserved tenant ID. Tenant create checks only the §10.2 pattern, and `jwtaudit.PlatformTenantID` is the literal "platform". A platform-admin can create a tenant named `platform` and then delete it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenants.go:997, pkg/auth/auth.go:259-263, pkg/gateway/audit/jwtaudit/jwtaudit.go:37
WATCHOUT: the new tenant `audit` entry (Store table row `audit`) runs `DELETE FROM audit_log WHERE tenant_id=$1 AND event_type NOT LIKE 'gdpr.%'`. For a tenant named `platform` it would erase the platform chain. That chain holds every tenant's `legal_hold.escrowed` and `escrow_released` ledger events (CODE-5 item 3), every TENANT_NOT_FOUND auth_failure row (CODE-6 item 4), and the impersonation and `security.audit_write_rejected` events. CODE-5 item 3's "so the tenant `audit` entry never deletes it" is therefore false for that one tenant ID. D4 and CODE-5 item 4 already concede that an admin can name a tenant `platform`, but only for escrow objects. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/auditstore.go:536-538, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:931
DECISION: I filed this as a non-spec finding. The suggested remedy has the `audit` entry return (0, nil) for `jwtaudit.PlatformTenantID`, pins that with a tier-1 case, and has REC-1 file the missing reservation of `platform` at tenant create — BECAUSE this is a reduction-style fail-closed guard that needs no spec edit — ALTERNATIVES: reserving `platform` at create with RESERVED_IDENTIFIER changes the admin API contract, so it needs a spec edit and is the owner's call. Pausing Phase 4 on an error would wedge that tenant forever.
FACT: the security lens found nothing else to file. CODE-6 item 6 discriminates by verifying key (production runs only the Token Service verifier, token_signing.go:132-160). The registry refusal fails closed on a lookup error. Every new Redis or Postgres read in Phase 3 and Phase 4 returns its error into pausePhase. No bound comes from a pod self-report. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:132-172, pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:470-485
FACT: the catalog `legal_hold = false` guard that CODE-3 item 10 drops protected only the catalog row. The s3 prefix delete removes held blobs without checking holds, so the Phase 3.5 preflight is the only hold gate both before and after the change. EVIDENCE: pkg/blobstore/s3/s3.go:607-636, pkg/blobstore/cataloging/cataloging.go:447-460

### [owner-decisions.2026-10-22]
DECISION: OD-3 resolved as D26: the separate T-10.2.11 direct fix lands before this proposal, renders the gateway's multi-tenant switch from the tenancy configuration, and makes single-tenant mode ignore the bearer's tenant claim; this proposal qualifies the Goal and D11 to multi-tenant mode, starts X1 with `--multi-tenant=true`, and files nothing for the branch — BECAUSE the owner chose a separate fix first (2026-10-22) — ALTERNATIVES: fixing the branch and chart here; wiring `bearerTenantRegistry` into the single-tenant branch; filing a **New** entry.
DECISION: OD-1 resolved as D27: in a multi-tenant deployment `default` is an ordinary tenant and may be deleted; SPEC-2 Edit 2b states the initial admin token refusal from Phase 3 and the bootstrap seed on upgrade, DOCS-1 item 6 tells operators what to move first, and the single-tenant refusal stays — BECAUSE the owner chose allow and document (2026-10-22) — ALTERNATIVES: refusing the deletion in multi-tenant mode; allowing it with no record.
DECISION: OD-2 resolved as D28: an admin `Router` built without `WithMultiTenant` behaves as single-tenant and refuses to delete `default` (CODE-6 item 5, T1.18) — BECAUSE the owner chose fail closed (2026-10-22) — ALTERNATIVES: a multi-tenant default that fails open.

### [non-spec.7.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 item 7 (retitled **Tenant listers after the tombstone.**) gives both listers one rule: add `default` only when the store has no `default` row. `tenantsLister` calls `Get("default")` only when its `ListFilter{}` listing lacks it, adds `default` on `ErrNotFound`, and returns any other `Get` error; T1.21 gains the tombstoned-`default`, missing-row, and `Get`-error cases — BECAUSE the unconditional prepend kept a deleted `default` (D27) in FlusherManager's set forever, and each replica started after the tombstone recreates `t:default:billing:stream` and its group through `ensureGroup` with no TTL — ALTERNATIVES: an Edge case 5 clause accepting the residue (unbounded in time); a flusher-only third lister (near-duplicate); unconditional `Get` or IncludeDeleted+filter (per-tick KMS decrypt or ~17 callers reading tombstones); FlusherManager on IncludeDeleted (already rejected in Traps "Exempt flush claim").
WATCHOUT: do not make `tenantsLister` call `Get("default")` unconditionally. `pgstore.Get` KMS-decrypts the salt of an active row on every call; the listing-lacks-`default` guard keeps the common case free. A `Get` error must be returned rather than turned into an omission, because FlusherManager.reconcile keeps its running set only on a lister error and would otherwise stop `default`'s flusher on a transient fault. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:98-114,267-281; pkg/gateway/billing/billingstore/failover/redisstream/flushermanager.go:102-135
FACT: `pgstore.Get` returns soft-deleted rows and `openSalt` returns nil for an empty blob, so a tombstoned `default` whose salt Phase 4 nulled reads without error. `Memory.Get` returns `ErrNotFound` only for a missing row. — EVIDENCE: pgstore.go:98-101,264-281; tenantstore.go:588-596
CORRECTS [review-log FACT "tenantsLister.ListTenants prepends default"]: true of the tree, but after CODE-3 item 7 the prepend is conditional on the store having no `default` row; read later entries that assume an always-listed `default` (idempotency GC, FlusherManager, sweepIssuedTokens) against item 7.
USEFUL [review-log FACT "tenantsLister.ListTenants prepends default"]: located the defect's source in one read of worker_seams.go:68-83.

### [non-spec.7.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: DOCS-1 item 6 now tells operators to set `gateway.adminToken.tenant`, upgrade, and rotate the initial admin token (pointing at installation.md), or to provision another human platform-admin, and separately to remove `default` from the bootstrap seed — BECAUSE `Provision` early-returns on an existing Secret and `sign` stamps `AdminTenant` only at mint time, so only `Rotate` re-scopes the `lenny-admin` token — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the admin-token clause (leaves a bootstrap Job reading `lenny-admin-token` failing every upgrade); re-mint in `Provision` on tenant mismatch (new mechanism, contradicts §17.6 "re-runs are a no-op"); naming `LENNY_ADMIN_TOKEN_TENANT` as the setting (chart renders it from the Helm value).
FACT: changing `--admin-token-tenant` does not re-scope the held `lenny-admin` token; only `POST /v1/admin/users/{user}/rotate-token` mints under the new tenant, and it must run before `default` is deleted because it authenticates with the old token. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go:240-252 (Provision early return), :431-447 (sign), charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:756-757, spec/17_deployment-topology.md:673, docs/operator-guide/installation.md:372-375
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2 Edit 2b glosses the `lenny-admin` tenant as "the gateway's `--admin-token-tenant`"; after a tenant change without rotation the held token's tenant differs from the setting. Not edited in the non-spec loop. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edit 2b
CORRECTS [standing context "Bootstrap admin in multi-tenant (D27)"]: DOCS-1 item 6 no longer "tells operators what to move first"; it holds set-upgrade-rotate steps plus the seed removal. The review-log line should read "DOCS-1 item 6 holds the operator steps".

### [non-spec.7.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the deleted-`default` flusher finding in code: CODE-3 item 7 states one rule for both listers (add `default` only when the store has no `default` row; `tenantsLister` reads the row with `Get` only when its `ListFilter{}` listing lacks `default`). Edge case 5 then holds for `default` unchanged — BECAUSE the residue is unbounded in time (every replica start recreates the stream and group) and the lister fix is a few lines in a file CODE-3 already edits; it also removes the duplicate `default` both listers return on Postgres — ALTERNATIVES: an Edge case 5 clause accepting the residue (records an unbounded departure the owner's "bounded departures" rule does not cover); a third flusher-only lister (hair); FlusherManager on IncludeDeleted (already rejected in Traps).
CORRECTS [Settled, FlusherManager]: "`tenantsLister` with `ListFilter{}` drops deleted rows, so a tombstoned stream is never flushed again" was false for `default`, which `tenantsLister` prepends unconditionally. After a D27 deletion, default's flusher ran forever and a post-tombstone replica's `ensureGroup` (`XGroupCreateMkStream`) recreated `t:default:billing:stream`. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/worker_seams.go:72-80, redisstream/flushermanager.go:106-131, redisstream.go:242,280-286
FACT: with Postgres, `tenantsLister` today returns `default` twice (prepended plus the migration-0053/0054 row); FlusherManager dedupes via its `want` map, but other callers visit `default` twice per tick. EVIDENCE: worker_seams.go:72-80, tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:368-392
WATCHOUT: `pgstore.Store.Get` KMS-decrypts `erasure_salt` when present, so a `tenantsLister` that calls `Get("default")` on every tick costs a KMS decrypt per caller per tick on an active `default`. Read the row only when the listing lacks `default`. A tombstoned `default` has its salt nulled (CODE-3 item 6), so that read is cheap. EVIDENCE: pgstore.go:98-110,267-281
WATCHOUT: `tenantsLister` has about 17 production callers (retention GC, orphan sweeps, watchdog, quota, HPA, store_router rehydrate, sweepIssuedTokens). After the fix, all of them stop visiting a tombstoned `default`, as they already do for every other tombstoned tenant. No caller needs `default` after Phase 4. Billing retention keeps it through `allTenantsLister`. EVIDENCE: grep `tenantsLister{` cmd/lenny-gateway
WATCHOUT: the bootstrap seed's `--force-update` path clears `DeletedAt` on a tombstone, which puts a deleted `default` back into `tenantsLister` and restarts its flusher. That is the summary's filed **New** seed defect, and this fix does not cover it. EVIDENCE: summary.md:125

### [non-spec.7.fix-design-G2.1]
FACT: changing `--admin-token-tenant` never re-mints the token already in `lenny-admin-token`. `Provision` runs `ensureUser` then returns early when the Secret exists; only `Rotate` mints into an existing Secret, and `sign` stamps `p.cfg.AdminTenant` at mint time. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go:240-252, 279-298, 431-447
FACT: the chart renders `LENNY_ADMIN_TOKEN_TENANT` from Helm value `gateway.adminToken.tenant` (default `default`), so on a chart install the operator sets the value rather than the env var. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:756-757, charts/lenny/values.yaml:2617-2618
FACT: after `AdminTenant` moves, `Rotate`'s `ensureUser` creates `lenny-admin` under the new tenant, and the revoke of the old `default`-scoped jti is a silent no-op, because `RevokeWithAudit` filters `WHERE jti=$1 AND tenant_id=$2` and `adminIssuedTokens.DurableRevoke` maps ErrNotFound to nil. The old token stays live until Phase 3 of the `default` deletion refuses it, or until it expires. — EVIDENCE: admintoken.go:376-381,387-409; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:291-316; pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go:408
DECISION: DOCS-1 item 6 replaces the "move the admin token tenant" clause with this sequence: set `gateway.adminToken.tenant`, upgrade, and then rotate through the existing rotation steps in docs/operator-guide/installation.md. The edit replaces the clause in place, and "or provision another human platform-admin" stays. — BECAUSE rotation is the only path that mints under the new tenant, and it also refreshes a bootstrap Job whose operatorTokenSecret is `lenny-admin-token`. — ALTERNATIVES: delete the clause and keep only "another platform-admin" (rejected: the rotation path is the only one that keeps both `lenny-admin` and a Job that reads its Secret working); a gateway change that re-mints on a tenant mismatch at Provision (rejected: new mechanism, contradicts §17.6 "re-runs are a no-op").
OPEN: a rotation after `--admin-token-tenant` changes leaves the predecessor jti unrevoked (tenant-mismatch no-op). This contradicts the §17.6 rotation guarantee in general, although the `default` deletion bounds it here. It is a candidate New defect or direct fix and outside G2.
WATCHOUT: Edit 2b's gloss "(... the gateway's `--admin-token-tenant`, `default` unless set)" identifies the tenant holding `lenny-admin` by the current setting. Between a setting change and a rotation, the Secret's token and the old user row still sit in the previous tenant. This is a separate spec-loop finding at most and is not made wrong by G2. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:48

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]

DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 7. BECAUSE the only hunks since r5-prefix (T1.12's zero-`DeletedAt` `deleted` row, T2.8 over T1.12's table, and X1's `--tenancy-mode=multi`) add no per-request, per-session, or per-task write, no watch, and no new store dependency. The tenancy-mode × token-type × tenant-state rows give the same per-request cost as the shipped non-default posture: in single-tenant mode there is no registry read, and in multi-tenant mode there is one uncached primary `Get` per bearer, which `default` now pays too. ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the D12 platform-chain contention (refuted in many earlier rounds); re-filing the `default` Postgres-failover refusal (non-spec.4 WATCHOUT; fail-closed matches every other tenant).

### [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no finding this round. BECAUSE the r5-to-now delta (T1.12 zero-DeletedAt `deleted` row, T2.8 over T1.12's table, X1 `--tenancy-mode=multi --multi-tenant=true`) adds no retry, resume, or reclaim path. Every recovery path I re-traced is either idempotent under rerun and leader overlap or bounded by a recorded reclaimer. The paths traced are Phase 3 commit-then-lease-then-TokenStore after a takeover, CODE-8 pausePhase versus pauseDrain State handling, the CODE-5 item 6 re-entry skip keyed on `ArtifactURI`, the CODE-9 commit-then-error re-read, a full_revoke repeat on an already-revoked user, and a Phase 4 rerun after `tenant_config`. ALTERNATIVES: (1) the terminal r.fail on the new CODE-5 Phase 3.5 errors. A leader change recovers it, because ensureJob rebuilds at Phase 3, and it is already the **New** "Phases other than 2, 3, and 4" entry. (2) A CODE-9 commit-then-error pre-running session that gets no ReclaimRevokedPreRunning. The orphan-claim GC bounds it, and the Non-goals weigh it. (3) A stale old-leader syncBack that writes `disabling` over Phase 3's committed `deleting`. It lasts at most one tick of an ex-leader, predates this proposal, and belongs to the security lens.
FACT: tenantstore pgstore `Update` and `Get` both call `openSalt` (a KMS envelope Open under `saltKEKAlias(tenantID)`) whenever the row carries a salt. So CODE-8 step 1, CODE-3 item 3, syncBack, and every `bearerTenantRegistry` lookup need KMS until `tenant_config` nulls the salt. A rerun after Phase 4a still works because the salt is already NULL by then. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:98-114,267-281,285-303
FACT: `userstore` pgstore `Update` selects without a `deleted_at` filter, so a full_revoke repeated after a replica crash (spec Edge case 4) reaches the session loop again instead of answering 404. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:117
FACT: `Migrate` skips a blobless hold (`BlobURI == ""`) before any read. The ledger saves `ArtifactURI` = `rec.URI` on both the artifact branch and CODE-5 item 5's session branch, so `ActiveForArtifact(tenant, h.ArtifactURI)` matches on re-entry. EVIDENCE: pkg/legalholdescrow/migrator.go:203-240; cmd/lenny-gateway/legalholdescrow.go:144-160,301-331
FACT: `credassign.Client.Release` removes the local lease record even when the RevokeCredentials RPC fails, and it drops the renewal entry. So with the deny-list write gone, a failed RPC in the Phase 3 or full_revoke lease step still rejects the token, and the Token Service lease ages out at its TTL. `Service.Release` (in-process) cannot fail. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:363-376; credassign.go:400-405
FACT: `Memory.SoftDelete` and pgstore `SoftDelete` both set `state = 'deleted'` together with `deleted_at`, so T1.12's "deleted (built by SoftDelete)" row is the both-halves case, and the `Update`-built row is the State-only case. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/tenantstore.go:638-657; pgstore/pgstore.go:396-402
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]: its FACTs that pgstore Update has no deleted_at filter and that tenantsLister prepends `default` saved re-deriving the rerun-after-tombstone cases.
USEFUL [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]: its FACT that Phase 3.5 re-entry after the `artifacts` entry sees no catalog-derived artifact holds saved re-tracing the hold enumerator.

### [non-spec.7.review-security.1]

DECISION: no findings. BECAUSE the tenant-resolution table (gateway mode after the T-10.2.11 fix, by Token Service bearer, embedded HMAC, playground bearer, dev header, lenny-ops SA, and lenny-admin token, by active/disabling/deleting/deleted/missing/store-error and `platform`) agrees with CODE-6, Edit 2b, SPEC-3 (3b, 3e), X1, and T1.18 in every row. FACT: The T-10.2.11 fix (branch fix/t-10.2.11-multi-tenant-flag, commit eb508cef5, not yet merged) adds `applyTenantAuthOptions` in cmd/lenny-gateway/tenancy.go. Multi-tenant mode wires `bearerTenantRegistry` and checks the dev header against it. Single-tenant mode sets Registry nil, and in dev mode `DevHeaderNamesTenant` lets X-Lenny-Tenant-ID name any tenant with no registry check. `validateTenancyConfig` fails startup unless `--multi-tenant` is true exactly when `--tenancy-mode=multi`. X1's `--tenancy-mode=multi --multi-tenant=true` with the `default` header passes. EVIDENCE: git show eb508cef5:cmd/lenny-gateway/tenancy.go
FACT: The CMP-058 residency gate resolves a not-found target tenant to "" and writes to the global platform-Postgres (rule 2). It names forged-tenant rejections in its scope. CODE-6 item 4's platform-chain TENANT_NOT_FOUND row therefore does not fail closed for an unregistered claim, and a deleting tenant with a region routes to that region. This answers the Open "UNVERIFIED D12 residency" entry. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/residency.go:17-38,70-74
FACT: In production the bearer verifier is only the Token Service RotatingVerifier. The embedded HMAC member exists only with `--bearer-trust-hmac-key-file`, which requires `--dev-mode`. CODE-6 item 6's configured-claim wrapper therefore changes dev-mode behavior only. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:132-160
WATCHOUT: The playground OIDC cookie mint (`mintOIDC`) mints a bearer from the Redis session record with no registry check, and `completeMint` appends `playground.bearer_minted` to the tenant's own chain. A cookie session established before Phase 3 can therefore add a non-`gdpr.%` row to a deleted tenant's chain after the audit eraser, within `playground.oidcSessionTtlSeconds` (default 3600). Phase 4 also never erases the `t:{tenant_id}:pg:*` keys, because the §12.8 scope table does not name them; they are TTL-bounded. This is pre-existing, and the minted bearer is refused at use. If X1 obtains its playground bearer through the cookie path after the audit eraser, the "no non-gdpr row" assertion fails. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go:86-141,323, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:198
OPEN: Should the playground session backing store (`t:{tenant_id}:pg:*`) join the §12.8 erasure scope, or be added to the summary's New entry for tenant-keyed stores that neither the scope table nor `canonicalRank` names? This is a later round's or a human's call.
WATCHOUT: The chart's bootstrap Job dev-header fallback sends `X-Lenny-Tenant-ID: platform` (values.yaml `bootstrap.auth.devTenant: platform`). On a multi-tenant dev-mode gateway after T-10.2.11, that header is checked against the registry, and `platform` has no row, so the Job is refused. Edit 3e also stops an operator from creating a `platform` row as a workaround. This belongs to the T-10.2.11 owner rather than to 0095; record it there if it matters. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:4148, charts/lenny/templates/bootstrap-job.yaml:78

### [redesign.2026-10-22]
DECISION: R1. `upsertTenants` records a seeded tenant whose row is in `deleting` or `deleted`, or has `DeletedAt` set, as an `error` entry with `SEED_VALIDATION`, with and without `--force-update`, and runs no KMS probe, update, or sequence provisioning for it; the force-update `DeletedAt` clear is deleted (CODE-6 item 8, T1.24, S11, summary D29, Edit 2b's seed sentence and spec Edge case 5 rewritten) — BECAUSE the clear was the only path in the tree that returns a tombstone to `tenantsLister`, and with the round-7 lister fix a revived tombstone re-entered every worker and its flusher recreated the purged billing stream — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the filed **New** revival defect with a D27 bound (rejected by the owner: an unbounded re-entry into every worker); restoring `State` on revival (revives a tenant whose data Phase 4 erased). Owner-delegated, 2026-10-22.
DECISION: R2. CODE-6 item 5 keys the `default` deletion refusal on the admin Router's existing `Options.TenancyMode`; any value other than `multi`, an empty one included, refuses. The `WithMultiTenant` setter, its `buildAdminRouter` wiring, and its `release_wiring_test.go` row are dropped, and T1.18 runs over `single`, empty, and `multi` (summary D28 amended, non-goal added, CODE-6 Targets and the summary index no longer name `adminrouter.go`) — BECAUSE `--tenancy-mode` already reaches the Router and, since T-10.2.11, cannot contradict `--multi-tenant` at startup, so a second tenancy input added nothing; reading an unset value as single-tenant keeps D28's fail-closed default — ALTERNATIVES: the setter (a redundant input); keying on `--multi-tenant` through a new option (same redundancy). Owner-delegated, 2026-10-22.
DECISION: R3. The T-10.2.11 direct fix is written in as landed context (merge 2177ac596): D26, the Watch-out bullet, the defects list, and the review-log Settled and Traps entries describe it as landed, the checklist precondition reads "satisfied", and DOCS-1 item 7 reconciles the step 2 sentence the fix added to multi-tenancy.md with Edit 3b — BECAUSE the fix is an ancestor of this tree — ALTERNATIVES: keeping it as a forward dependency (false of the tree). Owner-delegated, 2026-10-22.
DECISION: the liveness and default-tenant decisions get one home, the non-spec subsection **Tenant liveness and the default tenant** after CODE-6, holding the predicate (listed, registered, accepts new work, and the missing-`default` fallback), a 24-row site table derived from the tree before the staging was mapped, and the `tenantsLister` caller table for the new `Get` error. D11, D27, D28, D29, Edit 2b, DOCS-1 item 6, Edge case 5, CODE-3 item 7, and CODE-6 items 1 to 3, 5, and 8 point to it — BECAUSE the late findings were one mechanism no lens owned, decided ad hoc at many sites — ALTERNATIVES: a summary decision holding the table (the summary indexes decisions rather than sites).
DECISION: unhandled rows were filed rather than staged: row 5 (playground cookie mint runs no registry check, and Phase 4 erases no `t:{tenant_id}:pg:*` key), row 12 (tenant-row writers outside the lifecycle accept a `deleting` or `deleted` row, so a write after `tenant_config` puts back a nulled field), and row 23 (the admin-token provisioner recreates the `lenny-admin` user row in a deleted admin tenant) became summary **New** entries that REC-1 item 8 files; row 24 (request-path fallbacks to `default` with no principal) is owned by the in-flight F-10.2.21 direct fix — BECAUSE each needs a behavior or error-code choice that no staged spec text backs, and none blocks an intent of this proposal once X1 mints its playground bearer before Phase 3 — ALTERNATIVES: staging a registry check in `mintOIDC` (needs a §27.3.1 mint-path rule); a `Registered()` guard in `handleRotateErasureSalt` (needs an error code for `deleting`).

### [spec.8.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: Phase 4 `TokenIssuanceStore` entry (SPEC-5 Edit 5c) points at step 18's keep rule instead of deleting every row of the tenant — BECAUSE single-tenant mode ignores the bearer claim (spec/10 §10.2 tenant-claim table; pkg/auth/auth.go `if !r.MultiTenant`), so an impersonation bearer minted for a non-default tenant (impersonation.go checks only tenant existence) stays presentable as `default` after its tenant's Phase 4; deleting its revoked row loses the revocation on rehydration and breaks §13.3 "sole authoritative store" — ALTERNATIVES: forbid non-default tenants in single-tenant mode (new product rule across sections); single-tenant liveness check in §10.2 (round 2 withdrew it); delete only unrevoked rows (Revoke then returns ErrNotFound, the defect step 18 closed); wire the eraser to DeleteExpired (splits the predicate across methods).

### [spec.8.review-security.1]

A single-tenant deployment can create and delete non-default tenants (no tenancy gate in handleCreateTenant), and a platform-admin can mint a bearer whose tenant_id is such a tenant (impersonation requires only that the target tenant row exists, max 1h by default). The single-tenant gateway serves that bearer as `default`. ### [redesign3.2026-10-22]
DECISION: single-tenant bound. SPEC-2 Edit 2a, the spec Design bullet, spec Edge case 1, and DOCS-1 item 1 scope the Phase 3 bearer refusal to multi-tenant mode, and the non-spec **Tenant liveness and the default tenant** paragraph **Tenancy mode** states once what bounds a deleted non-`default` tenant in single-tenant mode: every request outside dev mode runs as `default`, so its bearers reach none of its rows, and its revocations survive Phase 4 through the §13.3 retention rule and the `allTenantsLister` rehydration. Rows 6 and 22 now cite that paragraph instead of "row 7 keeps `default` active", and D11 points to it — BECAUSE §10.2's single-tenant row ignores the claim and serves `default` (`ExtractTenant` returns `default` with no registry read), the F-10.2.21 fix (merge a39e2c6df, now in this tree) makes outside-dev-mode requests need a bearer and take the tenant only from the principal, and CODE-6 item 5 refuses only `default`, so the unconditional "rejects every bearer token of the tenant" sentence was false in single-tenant mode and contradicted D11 — ALTERNATIVES: refusing creation of non-`default` tenants in single-tenant mode (rejected: no spec site ties tenant creation to the tenancy mode, the dev and integration suites create tenants on single-tenant dev gateways per `applyTenantAuthOptions`, and it bounds nothing further because a single-tenant request cannot select a tenant outside dev mode); a registry check on the single-tenant bearer path (rejected in round 2, Traps).
DECISION: the dev-mode residue is accepted and stated in **Tenancy mode** and row 6: under `DevHeaderNamesTenant` the `X-Lenny-Tenant-ID` header names any well-formed tenant, a deleted one included, and nothing is staged — BECAUSE dev mode trusts unauthenticated identity headers by design and wiring the registry there would refuse the uncreated tenants the dev suites name — ALTERNATIVES: a registry check on the dev-header path (breaks the suites; a dev-mode-only exposure).
DECISION: the issued-token retention rule has one spec home, new SPEC-5 Edit 5d in §13.3 **Authoritative durability for revocation**, inserted after "(the trailing hour covers tokens that expired after revocation and may still be presented by a buggy client).", covering user erasure, tenant Phase 4, and the expired-row GC (all tenants, a deleted one included). Edits 5a, 5b (step 18), and 5c cite it; step 18 loses its keep sentence. D9, D25, the CODE-4 index line, CODE-4 items 3 and 4, the Store table `issued_tokens` cells, spec Edge case 2, and a new spec Design bullet cite it; the SPEC-5 heading, "Spec files touched", the summary SPEC-5 index line, and checklist S5 name spec/13 — BECAUSE the rule governs both erasure paths and the GC while its only home was the user-path step 18, which the tenant path borrowed — ALTERNATIVES: the §12.2 `TokenIssuanceStore` row (a role table; §13.3 already owns the trailing hour and the "sole authoritative store" property the rule serves); keeping step 18 as home with a widened subject (a user-erasure step stating a tenant-path rule).
DECISION: T1.25 (tier 1, lands with CODE-3) pins the §13.3 rehydration on `allTenantsLister`: a rehydration through `allTenantsLister` over a store with a tombstoned tenant reports that tenant's revoked `jti`, one through `tenantsLister` does not, and `release_wiring_test.go` rows pin `allTenantsLister{w.tenants}` at the `w.revCache.Rehydrate` calls and `tenantsLister{w.tenants}` at the §4.9 deny-list rebuild — BECAUSE spec.8.follow-up-fix.1 deferred the security half of the rehydration fix with no test, and both calls currently share the text `lister := tenantsLister{w.tenants}` in billing_security_workers.go — ALTERNATIVES: a wiring row only (does not show the lister choice changes the revocation outcome).
DECISION: row 24, summary non-goal "Restaging the request-path fallbacks", and the summary F-10.2.21 entry describe F-10.2.21 as closed in merge a39e2c6df — BECAUSE the merge of proposal-b/gateway-runtime-comms-remediation brought it into this tree (BUILD-GAPS.md marks it CLOSED; `authposture.go` sets `RequireAuth = !devMode`; `lookupTenant`, `translator.resolveTenant`, and the MCP tools take the tenant only from the principal outside dev mode) — ALTERNATIVES: none.
DECISION: checklist S4 reads "Lands the tenant deletion lifecycle edits and the §17.6 seed edits", closing the spec.8.fix-G1.1 and spec.8.fix-design-G1.1 DEFERRED entries; S5 names the §13.3 retention edit.
FACT: the Edit 5d find-string occurs once in spec/13_security-model.md; the other changed hunks (Edits 2a, 5a, 5b, 5c) changed only replacement text, so their find-strings are unchanged. — EVIDENCE: grep -cF over spec/13_security-model.md and spec/12_storage-architecture.md
FACT: the spec.8.follow-up-fix.1 DEFERRED (no test pins the rehydration lister) is closed by T1.25; the spec.8.fix-G1.1 and spec.8.fix-design-G1.1 DEFERRED entries on S4 are closed by the checklist edit.
WATCHOUT: the docs upsert-table OPEN from spec.8 (installation.md and lenny-ctl.md **Upsert Semantics** lack the seed refusal row; DOCS-1 does not target them) is still open for the non-spec loop. The UNVERIFIED `--admin-token-tenant` spec home (spec.8.review-client-surface.1) is also still open.

FACT: `w.llmLeases` is always non-nil (in-memory fallback), and `connectorCreds`/`credentials` stores always exist (memory fallback), so CODE-8's revoker never meets a nil lease lister or a nil `tokens` entry. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/executor_credentials.go:74,157; cmd/lenny-gateway/credentialsurface.go:72,140

(1) The backup crypto-shredding bullet's `DeleteByTenant` wrap-key destruction (spec/12 **Backups in erasure scope**) is absent from the Phase 4 order. It is pre-existing and distinct from the erasure_salt clause that Edit 5c drops. (2) The §10.2 format-validation boundary still says "at OIDC claim extraction time" after Edit 3a widens extraction to gateway tokens. Gateway tokens carry minted, already-valid IDs, so nothing breaks. (3) Edit 5c leaves the tenant-scoped `delegation_policies` table (migration 0054) out of Phase 4.
